--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed from the graph such that the two black nodes (R and P) are completely disconnected, we need to examine the paths between these two nodes and find the critical edges that, when removed, will disconnect them.

1. **Identify the black nodes:**
   - The black nodes are R and P.

2. **Identify the immediate neighbors of R and P:**
   - R is connected to B, J.
   - P is connected to Q, I.

3. **Find intermediate neighbors:**
   - Neighbors of B: A, E, S, T, F, C, H, N, O, G, J, Q.
   - Neighbors of J: B, C, H, Q.
   - Neighbors of Q: J, H, I, C.
   - Neighbors of I: G, H, Q, O.

4. **Paths from R to P:**
   - Path 1: R -> B -> J -> Q -> P
   - Path 2: R -> B -> J -> C -> Q -> P
   - Path 3: R -> B -> J -> H -> Q -> P
   - Path 4: R -> B -> J -> H -> I -> P
   - Path 5: R -> B -> C -> Q -> P
   - Path 6: R -> B -> C -> H -> Q -> P
   - Path 7: R -> B -> C -> H -> I -> P
   - Path 8: R -> B -> H -> Q -> P
   - Path 9: R -> B -> H -> I -> P

5. **Evaluate the paths for edges to remove:**
   - Common edges in all paths are (J, Q) and (Q, P).
   - Removing the edges (J, Q) and (Q, P) disconnects R and P.

Thus, the minimum number of edges that must be removed is 2.

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(J, Q), (Q, P)].

   - Node A
   - Node H

2. **Find the immediate neighbors of node A:**
   - Node A is connected to Node B, Node S, and Node E.

3. **Find the immediate neighbors of node H:**
   - Node H is connected to Node G and Node I.

   - Path 1: A → B → C → G → H
   - Path 2: A → B → F → T → G → H
   - Path 3: A → B → C → N → O
   - Path 4: A → E → F → J → Q → I → H
   - Path 5: A → E → F → T → G → H
   - Path 6: A → S → F → J → Q → I → H

   - Removing edge (B, C) will disconnect the path A → B → C → G → H.

   - Removing edge (I, H) will disconnect the paths that go through I.

   - Removing edge (B, F) will disconnect the paths that go through F.

   After removing (B, F) and (G, H), all paths between A and H are disconnected.

To solve this problem, we need to identify the paths between the two black nodes, \( N \) and \( Q \), and determine the minimum number of edges that need to be removed to ensure that there are no paths connecting these two nodes.

1. **Identify the two black nodes:**
   - \( N \) and \( Q \) are the black nodes that need to be disconnected.

2. **Find all direct and indirect neighbors of both black nodes:**
   - Direct neighbors of \( N \): \( C \)
   - Direct neighbors of \( Q \): \( P \), \( I \)

3. **Trace the paths from \( N \) to \( Q \):**
   - \( N \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \rightarrow O \rightarrow R \rightarrow Q \)
   - \( N \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \rightarrow J \rightarrow I \rightarrow Q \)
   - \( N \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \rightarrow J \rightarrow R \rightarrow Q \)
   - \( N \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \rightarrow J \rightarrow O \rightarrow R \rightarrow Q \)

   In a more detailed breakdown:
   - \( N \rightarrow C \)
   - \( C \rightarrow G \)
   - \( G \rightarrow O \), \( G \rightarrow J \), \( G \rightarrow R \)
   - \( O \rightarrow R \)
   - \( J \rightarrow I \)
   - Multiple combinations which show interconnectedness through various paths

4. **Identify the critical edges for disconnection:**
   - By examining the paths, removing the edges \( (C, G) \) will cut through multiple paths simultaneously.

5. **Evaluate the minimal set of edges to be removed:**
   - Removing \( (C, G) \) alone is not enough because \( N \) and \( Q \) may still have other paths.
   - Therefore, we need to consider removing \( (N, C) \) along with \( (C, G) \).

6. **Verify the solution:**
   - Removing \( (N, C) \) and \( (C, G) \):
     - This effectively cuts off all possible paths from \( N \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \) to any connections related to \( Q \).

After careful examination, we conclude that the minimum number of edges that must be removed is 2. The edges to be removed are \( (N, C) \) and \( (C, G) \).

   - Removing (N, H) disconnects another path going through N.

### Analysis of the Graph

1. **Identify the Black Nodes:**
   - The black nodes are labeled A and T.

2. **Immediate Neighbors of A:**
   - Node A connects directly to nodes B, E, and N.

3. **Immediate Neighbors of T:**
   - Node T connects directly to nodes C, G, and S.

4. **Nodes Reachable from A:**
   - From A to B: B connects to E, N, S, Q, I, and J.
   - From A to E: E connects to N, S, F, Q, T, C, and I.
   - From A to N: N connects to S, O, and T.

5. **Nodes Reachable from T:**
   - From T to C: C connects to E, G, and H.
   - From T to G: G connects to H.
   - From T to S: S connects to A, E, F, N, and O.

6. **Finding the Paths:**
   - Path 1: A -> E -> T
   - Path 2: A -> N -> T (and O -> S -> E -> T)
   - Path 3: A -> B -> Q -> I -> J -> R
   - Note: E -> C -> G -> H is also a path, but does not connect directly to A unless through E -> T or S -> T.

### Review of the Cut Perspectives:

- **Direct Edges Removal Perspective:**
  - (A, E) and (E, T) need to be removed.
  - (A, N) and (N, T) need to be removed, but since (N, T) isn't directed, indirect paths through S must be considered.

- **Optimized Path:**
  - To completely disconnect A and T:
    - Remove (A, E) and (E, T) to remove direct link.
    - Consider the indirect paths through E to cut this path requires at least a combination of intermediate cuts.

### Final Cut Set:
- **Minimum Cuts to Ensure Complete Disconnection:**
  - (A, E), (E, T), and removing the intermediate cross-node connections reveals:
    - *B*otleneck: Removing three edges is sufficient.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges that need to be removed to completely disconnect the black nodes A and T is:

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(A, E), (E, T), (N, T)].

--- Model Response ---
To address the problem, we need to identify the shortest paths between the two black nodes (E, J) and then remove the minimum number of edges to disconnect them.

First, we will identify all paths between nodes E and J:

1. Path: E -> B -> N -> H -> I -> Q -> J
2. Path: E -> B -> N -> H -> A -> Q -> J
3. Path: E -> B -> N -> H -> I -> S -> Q -> J
4. Path: E -> B -> N -> H -> I -> P -> A -> Q -> J
5. Path: E -> B -> N -> H -> A -> P -> I -> S -> Q -> J

We can observe from these paths that several edges form critical bridges. Specifically, these paths all go through B, N, H, and Q. By strategically removing these critical edges, we can ensure that the two black nodes are disconnected.

Now, let's determine the minimal number of edges that must be removed to achieve complete disconnection:

1. Removing edge (E, B) disconnects E from its direct connection to B.
2. Removing edge (B, N) disconnects B from N.
3. Removing edge (N, H) disconnects N from H.
4. Removing edge (Q, J) disconnects Q from J.

By removing these 4 edges, all paths between E and J are severed. However, we can optimize further by considering the critical points:

1. Removing the edge (E, B) plus (B, N) already eliminates the paths through these nodes:
   - E -> B -> N -> H -> I -> Q -> J
   - E -> B → other paths through N, H, and Q.

2. Removing the edge (Q, J) undoubtedly disconnects Q from J.

This combination means removing three edges suffices:

Edges to remove: 3
Cut Set: (E, B), (B, N), (Q, J)

**Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(E, B), (B, N), (Q, J)].**

{'length': 1, 'cut_set': [['H', 'I']]}

- Node I is connected to nodes H and J.
- Node O is connected to nodes C, R, and Q.

   - Path: [I, H, G, C, O]
   - Length: 4

   - Path: [I, J, C, O]
   - Length: 3

3. From I to J, then from J to G, then from G to C, then from C to O:
   - Path: [I, J, G, C, O]
   - Length: 4

4. From I to J, then from J to R, then from R to O:
   - Path: [I, J, R, O]
   - Length: 3

From the paths found, the shortest paths are [I, J, C, O] and [I, J, R, O], both with a length of 3.

1. **Direct Paths:**
   - A -> B -> H

2. **Indirect Paths:**
   - A -> B -> J -> H
   - A -> B -> I -> H
   - A -> B -> R -> J -> H
   - A -> B -> R -> Q -> I -> H
   - A -> B -> R -> Q -> P -> G -> O -> N -> C -> H
   - A -> B -> C -> H

Now, let's identify the minimum set of edges to remove to disconnect these paths:

- Removing (A, B) will disconnect all paths passing through B.
- Removing (B, J) and (H, J) will disconnect the paths passing through J.
- Removing (B, I) and (H, I) will disconnect the paths passing through I.
- Removing (B, R) will disconnect the paths passing through R indirectly.
- Removing (B, C) and (C, H) will disconnect the paths passing through C.

However, the most efficient way is to remove the crucial edges that affect multiple paths:

- Remove (A, B): This disconnects A from all paths involving B.

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (E and N), follow these steps:

1. **Identify the black nodes:** The black nodes are E and N.
2. **Trace the paths between E and N:**
    - E is directly connected to A, B, and T.
    - N is directly connected to C, O, and R.
3. **Find all paths from E to N:**
    - Path 1: E -> B -> C -> N
    - Path 2: E -> B -> G -> J -> I -> H -> T -> F -> B -> C -> N
    - Path 3: E -> A -> B -> C -> N
    - Path 4: E -> T -> F -> B -> C -> N
    - Path 5: E -> A -> S -> B -> C -> N
    - Path 6: E -> T -> F -> B -> G -> J -> I -> H -> B -> C -> N

4. **Identify bottlenecks:**
    - The critical edges that, if removed, completely disconnect E and N are (B, C) and (E, B).

    Removing (B, C) breaks the main connection between E and N through B and C.
    Removing (E, B) directly disconnects E from the rest of the paths involving B.

5. **Check if removing these edges suffices:**
    - Removing (B, C) and (E, B) ensures that there are no remaining paths between E and N.

6. **Verify the minimal number of edges:**
    - There are no other combinations of fewer edges that can achieve the same disconnection.

**Output Format:**

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(B, C), (E, B)].
